What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because I'm feeling completely overwhelmed by this constant, intense fear of germs and contamination. I also spend hours wiping down surfaces in my house over and over again to make sure they are completely clean. But these rituals and thoughts simply take up a lot of my time every single day, making it really hard to get anything else done.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other significant symptoms or difficulties, such as depressed mood, anxiety in social situations, or excessive worry about things beyond germs and contamination?\n\nPatient: I don't really struggle with depression or social anxiety, but I definitely have severe worries that go far beyond just germs. I'm constantly plagued by pathological doubts, like whether I locked the front door or turned off the gas valve, and I also get terrifying, intrusive thoughts of getting a serious illness or even harming others. It feels like my mind is always finding something new to worry about.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe in more detail these terrifying, intrusive thoughts of harming others, and how they relate to your compulsive behaviors, such as washing your hands or cleaning?\n\nPatient: I get these horrible, sudden images of hurting the people I love, which makes me feel incredibly guilty and ashamed. Sometimes I wash my hands repeatedly just to try and \"cleanse\" myself of these awful thoughts, though they mostly feel like a separate, terrifying weight on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you tried any strategies or techniques to manage or cope with these obsessive thoughts and compulsive behaviors, and if so, have they provided any relief?"

What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because I'm feeling completely overwhelmed by this constant, intense fear of germs and contamination. It has gotten to the point where I feel forced to wash my hands and clean things over and over again just to feel safe. It's taking over my life and I don't know what to do.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about these repetitive behaviors, such as washing your hands and cleaning, and how often you feel the need to perform them?\n\nPatient: I feel forced to wash my hands dozens of times a day, often scrubbing them until they are raw because I'm terrified of germs. I also spend hours wiping down surfaces in my house over and over again to make sure they are completely clean. It feels like my mind is always finding something new to worry about.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe in more detail these terrifying, intrusive thoughts of harming others, and how they relate to your compulsive behaviors, such as washing your hands or cleaning?\n\nPatient: I get these horrible, sudden images of hurting the people I love, which makes me feel incredibly guilty and ashamed. Sometimes I wash my hands repeatedly just to try and \"cleanse\" myself of these awful thoughts, though they mostly feel like a separate, terrifying weight on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you tried any strategies or techniques to manage or cope with these obsessive thoughts and compulsive behaviors, and if so, have they provided any relief?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I haven't really found any effective ways to manage these thoughts and behaviors on my own. The only thing that gives me any temporary relief is giving in to the compulsions and performing the rituals. Other than that, nothing seems to help stop them.\n\nDoctor: Have you sought or received any professional help, such as therapy or medication, for these symptoms before, and if so, what was your experience with those treatments?"

But these rituals and thoughts simply take up a lot of my time every single day, making it really hard to get anything else done.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other significant symptoms or difficulties, such as depressed mood, anxiety in social situations, or excessive worry about things beyond germs and contamination?\n\nPatient: I don't really struggle with depression or social anxiety, but I definitely have severe worries that go far beyond just germs. I'm constantly plagued by pathological doubts, like whether I locked the front door or turned off the gas valve, and I also get terrifying, intrusive thoughts of getting a serious illness or even harming others. It feels like my mind is always finding something new to worry about.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe in more detail these terrifying, intrusive thoughts of harming others, and how they relate to your compulsive behaviors, such as washing your hands or cleaning?\n\nPatient: I get these horrible, sudden images of hurting the people I love, which makes me feel incredibly guilty and ashamed. Sometimes I wash my hands repeatedly just to try and \"cleanse\" myself of these awful thoughts, though they mostly feel like a separate, terrifying weight on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you tried any strategies or techniques to manage or cope with these obsessive thoughts and compulsive behaviors, and if so, have they provided any relief?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I haven't really found any effective ways to manage these thoughts and behaviors on my own. The only thing that gives me any temporary relief is giving in to the compulsions and performing the rituals. Other than that, nothing seems to help stop them.\n\nDoctor: Have you sought or received any professional help, such as therapy or medication, for these symptoms before, and if so, what was your experience with those treatments?\n\nPatient: No, I have never sought professional help, therapy, or medication for these symptoms before. This is actually my very first time reaching out to a doctor about what I've been experiencing.\n\nDoctor: Just to make sure I understand your situation correctly, can you summarize for me the main symptoms and challenges you're experiencing with your obsessive thoughts and compulsive behaviors, and how they're impacting your daily life?\n\nPatient: My main struggles are constant worries about contamination and safety doubts, which lead to exhausting rituals like excessive handwashing and constant checking. These repetitive behaviors consume hours of my time and drain almost all of my energy every day. It makes it incredibly difficult to focus on my daily tasks or just enjoy my life.\n\nDoctor: Now that we have a better understanding of your symptoms, I'd like to discuss potential treatment options with you. Would you be open to exploring therapies such as Cognitive-Behavioral Therapy (CBT) or medication to help manage your obsessive thoughts and compulsive behaviors?\n\nPatient: I would definitely be open to hearing more about CBT and medication, as I really want to find some relief. Could you explain a bit more about how these options would help with my specific symptoms, like the constant handwashing and checking?"

Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.
